# ASSET MANIFEST — Ashes of Empires: The Last Winter

Generated by `tools/docs/build_asset_manifest.py` from `documentation/asset_manifest_parts/`. Do not edit by hand;
edit the part of the owning area and re-run the script.

## Provenance rules (apply to every entry)
- **All art, audio, music, text and code are original work created for this project** by its generator scripts
  (`tools/pixelgen/*`, `tools/audiogen/*`) or written by hand. Every generator is deterministic: re-running it
  reproduces the files byte-for-byte.
- **The only third-party assets are four font families under the SIL Open Font License 1.1** (PT Serif, Oswald,
  Courier Prime, PT Mono — downloaded from the official google/fonts repository; licence texts ship next to them).
  See the UI part below for URLs and hashes.
- **No sample, texture, sound or image from the internet or from the reference images was copied, traced or
  embedded.** `documentation/references/` is an internal style study only, excluded from git and from the build.
- No purchased assets, no subscriptions, no AI-generated media from external services.
- The Great Hall and the "Reichssiegel" are fictional. Real regime insignia (swastika, state eagle, hammer & sickle,
  red star) exist only as `*_hist` variants of in-world assets, shown while the setting "Historische Hoheitszeichen"
  is on (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §12.4, StGB §86 Abs. 4 social-adequacy context); every one has a neutral twin.
- Real-history cards (`data/explore/history_*.json`, `data/locale/de_history_*.json`) are original German texts
  written from the sources listed on each card; they were researched and then independently fact-checked.

## Lead / integration entries
| Path | What | How it was made |
|---|---|---|
| `tools/pixelgen/pg.py` | shared pixel-art library (palettes, Bayer dither, layers with height→normal maps, volumetric puffs) | hand-written Python |
| `data/palettes.json`, `data/grades.json` | palettes and colour-grade presets | exported from `pg.PALETTES` / hand-authored |
| `assets/shaders/post_grade.gdshader` | post chain: palette snap (Bayer) → lift/gamma/gain → vignette → grain → fade | hand-written GLSL |
| `assets/icon/icon.png`, `icon_32.png`, `icon.ico` | window and executable icon (dome, stamp ring, one warm window) | `tools/pixelgen/icon.py` |
| `assets/environments/_test/*` | pipeline test environment (not shipped) | `tools/pixelgen/env_test.py` |

<a id="part-render-camera"></a>

## Asset manifest — RENDER / CAMERA (round 2: smooth camera at 60–360 Hz, crisp pixel art)

| | |
|---|---|
| Area owner | render-camera specialist |
| Date | 2026-11-04 (round 2) |
| Engine | Godot 4.7.2-stable, Forward+, GDScript |
| Provenance | **100 % original code.** No art or audio assets are produced by this area. The only images involved are throw-away test textures for `tests/visual/camera_decals.gd`, generated by a 10-line Pillow snippet into the scratch folder (never shipped). No downloads, no third-party code. |
| Licence | Project-owned original work (same terms as the game's own code). |
| Content policy | §12.4: every texture path loaded by `layer_stack.gd` (layers, props, decals, fx, their `_n`/`_e` maps) and by `sprite_rig.gd` (via `LayerStack.canvas_tex`) resolves through `Symbols.pick()`. New `"decals"` give environment owners a way to place insignia (neutral file + `_hist` variant) without baking them into layers. |

### Files
| Path | Role |
|---|---|
| `scenes/main/Main.tscn` | World (SubViewport) → **Graded** (new SubViewport: post stage, `GradeStage` TextureRect with the grade material) → WorldView/Display. `World/Post/Grade` keeps the same ShaderMaterial (Game writes it). |
| `scripts/core/main.gd` | Same public members (`world, level_root, camera, grade, view, ui_layer, overlay`) + `graded`. Switches PixelView to the low-res path while a Node3D level is in `LevelRoot` (signal fires before the level's `_ready`). |
| `scripts/render/pixel_view.gd` | Render paths, resolution (k), post-stage wiring, `capture_world_image()`, world-sprite material assignment + fade watch, CanvasLayer autoscale shim. |
| `scripts/render/world_camera.gd` | Smooth-damp follow, dead zone, look-ahead, pixel-grid quantisation, `draw_pos_for()` for the followed actor. |
| `scripts/render/texel_light.gd` (`class_name TexelLight`) | Material helper: coded (opaque) / plain (translucent) pixel-art materials, `apply(item, anchor, cell)`, `apply_tree()`, `set_blended()`. |
| `scripts/render/pixel_sprite.gdshader` | Opaque pixel-art sprites: `blend_disabled`, discard alpha < 0.5, per-texel lighting, **texel-anchored Bayer threshold written into alpha** (instance uniforms `dither_anchor`, `dither_cell`). |
| `scripts/render/texel_light.gdshader`, `texel_light.gdshaderinc` | Per-texel lighting (LIGHT_VERTEX moved to the texel centre) for translucent lit sprites; shared include. |
| `assets/shaders/post_grade.gdshader` | Grade / palette snap / dither / vignette / grain at any render scale; reads the texel dither codes; `from_texture` (post stage), `px_scale`, `dither_origin`, `grain_seed`, test hooks `dither_shift`, `debug_view` (1 threshold, 2 raw alpha). |
| `scripts/levels/layer_stack.gd` | Symbols.pick for all paths (+ `_hist_n`/`_hist_e` maps), `"decals"`, layer `blend`/`alpha`, fx `alpha`, parallax on the viewport pixel grid driven by `camera.moved`, dither anchors per layer/prop/decal. |
| `scripts/levels/fx_snow.gd` | Flakes in world-pixel sizes, positions on the viewport grid, parallax against camera motion (far 0.55, near 1.2). |
| `scripts/actors/player.gd` | Rendering only: render interpolation between physics states (`get_visual_position()`), rig drawn at `camera.draw_pos_for()`. Movement rules unchanged. |
| `scripts/levels/hall/hall_camera_rig.gd` | Lens shift applied in whole low-res px, remainder presented via `PixelView.set_fraction` (`cur_fraction`). |
| `tests/visual/camera_motion.gd/.tscn` + `camera_motion_analyze.py` | Motion / FPS / dither / capture / decal / hall round-trip harness (see header) and the offline analysis. |
| `tests/visual/camera_decals.gd/.tscn` | Headless structural check of decals + Symbols (run with `--symbols=0` and `--symbols=1`). |
| `tests/visual/camera_hall_rig_check.gd` | Headless lens-shift smoothness check of the hall rig. |
| `tests/visual/camera_probe`, `camera_snap_probe`, `camera_alpha_probe` (`.gd/.tscn`) | Engine probes (display facts; sub-pixel snapping rules; framebuffer alpha vs `hint_screen_texture`). Print only. |

### Rendering contract (replaces TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §2 for round 2)
```
Main (Node, main.gd)
 ├─ World (SubViewport)        hi-res: (640+2)k x (360+2)k, transparent, snap_2d_transforms OFF, vertices ON
 │   ├─ LevelRoot              levels / 2D cinematics / the 3D hall
 │   ├─ Camera (world_camera)  zoom = k, position = drawn - (1,1)   (1 world px overscan margin)
 │   └─ Post/Grade (ColorRect) in-viewport post: low-res path only (hidden in hi-res)
 ├─ Graded (SubViewport)       hi-res only: GradeStage (TextureRect = World texture, grade material, from_texture)
 ├─ WorldView/Display          shows Graded (hi-res) or World (low-res) at canvas -3,-3, size 1926x1086
 ├─ UI (CanvasLayer 10), Overlay (CanvasLayer 5)   unchanged
```
* **k = `Game.view.world_scale`** = round(3 × window content scale) when that is an integer and the stretch offset
  is whole (1080p → 3, 1440p → 4, 2160p → 6, 720p → 2): the world maps **1:1 onto physical pixels**. Other sizes:
  k = floor(3 × scale), stretched by the canvas (old behaviour). Re-evaluated on every window resize.
* **Camera**: `pos` continuous top-left (world px); `drawn` = `pos` rounded to 1/k world px; everything that is
  positioned by the render code lands on whole viewport pixels → camera, parallax and actors move in **single
  screen pixels**, texels stay crisp k×k blocks. `Game.world_to_ui()` unchanged (uses `drawn`).
* **Snapping**: Godot's `snap_2d_transforms_to_pixel` snaps in *world units* (verified by
  `camera_snap_probe`), so it is **off** in the hi-res path; `snap_2d_vertices_to_pixel` stays on. Free-moving
  nodes are rasterised on the nearest viewport pixel.
* **Low-res path** (any Node3D in LevelRoot, e.g. the Great Hall; also legacy `--capture-world` runs, see below):
  642×362, zoom 1, pixel-locked gameplay camera, sub-pixel remainder of cinematic moves and of the hall's lens
  shift via `PixelView.set_fraction` — identical to the round-1 pipeline.
* **Post** (`post_grade.gdshader`): palette snap + 4×4 Bayer, grade, vignette dither and grain are evaluated per
  world texel (`px_scale` = k, `dither_origin` = camera in viewport px). The dither threshold comes from the
  **texel code** that opaque sprites write into the framebuffer alpha (`pixel_sprite.gdshader`, 16 levels in
  0.03..0.87): the dither sticks to each layer's/actor's own texels → no dither crawl on parallax layers during
  pans, no 1-px slivers on actors between world pixels. Pixels without a code (alpha ≤ 0.02 or ≥ 0.97: other
  materials, translucent overlays over nothing, empty background) fall back to a playfield-anchored Bayer.
  `hint_screen_texture` copies force alpha = 1 (verified by `camera_alpha_probe`), hence the second stage.
* **World sprite materials** (assigned by PixelView to every Sprite2D/AnimatedSprite2D entering the World without
  a material of its own; layer_stack assigns them with plane-consistent `dither_anchor`): opaque binary-alpha
  textures → coded material (`TexelLight.coded_material()`); translucent lit sprites → per-texel lighting only.
  Coded sprites are watched every frame and fall back to normal blending while their effective modulate alpha
  < 1, so fades keep working. A node that brings its own material is never touched.
* **Followed actor**: `player.gd` interpolates its drawn position between the last two physics states
  (`get_visual_position()`), the camera chooses its pixel (`draw_pos_for`): it keeps its screen pixel while
  camera and player move together (TARGET_TOL 1 screen px), keeps its world pixel while standing and never steps
  back against its walking direction.
* **Follow model**: dead zone (6×4 px), look-ahead 40 px after 10 px of committed walking direction (own
  critically damped spring ω = 2.1/s, held when stopping), exact critically damped spring ω = 5.2/s (no
  overshoot, frame-rate independent). `reduced_motion`: ω ×2, look-ahead ×0.6 (ω ×2.2), dead zone ×0.6.
  `tween_to` keeps its API (Tween on `pos`, sub-pixel smooth); `release()` / `set_top_left()` stop a running
  `tween_to` tween.
* **Screen-space content inside the World** (a CanvasLayer under a level or cinematic laid out for 642×362) is
  scaled by k automatically (meta `pv_no_autoscale` opts out). `get_viewport().get_visible_rect()` inside the
  World returns the hi-res size — use `Game.view.world_scale` if a script needs it.

#### Public APIs (kept + new)
* Camera: `target, bounds, pos, drawn, VIEW, follow_offset, cinematic, set_bounds, clamp_pos, desired, snap_now,
  set_top_left, tween_to, release, top_left, moved` — new: `vel, look_ahead, dead_zone, smoothing,
  look_smoothing, px_scale(), draw_pos_for(node, visual), refresh()`.
* PixelView: `setup(vp, graded = null), set_fraction, world_to_ui, set_shake` — new: `is_hires(), set_hires(on),
  world_scale, capture_world_image() -> Image (640×360, graded), resolution_changed(k)`.
* Main: `world, level_root, camera, grade, view, ui_layer, overlay` — new: `graded`.
* LayerStack (static): `tex(path)` (now Symbols-resolved), `map_path(path, "_n"|"_e")`, `canvas_tex`,
  `light_texture`, `_v2`; instance: `layers_by_name`, `decals_by_name`.
* TexelLight: `coded_material(), material(), apply(item, anchor, cell), apply_tree(root, cell), set_blended(item,
  blended), is_coded(item), is_opaque_pixel_art(tex)`.

#### scene.json additions (layer_stack.gd)
```json
"layers": [ {"name": "shafts", "file": "shafts.png", "blend": "add", "alpha": 0.8, ...} ],
"fx":     [ {"type": "anim_sprite", "file": "fx/steam.png", "alpha": 0.85, ...} ],
"decals": [
  {"name": "banner_1", "file": "decals/decal_banner_1.png", "pos": [412, 96], "layer": "room", "z": 1},
  {"name": "plaque",   "file": "decals/decal_plaque.png",   "pos": [600, 250], "layer": "playfield", "base_y": 262},
  {"name": "poster",   "file": "decals/decal_poster.png",   "pos": [900, 40], "parallax": [0.45, 0.0], "z": -73,
   "lit": true, "normal": true, "emission": false, "emission_strength": 0.22, "alpha": 1.0, "flip_h": false,
   "frames": 1, "fps": 6}
]
```
* `file` relative to the scene.json folder, resolved through `Symbols.pick()` (`name_hist.png` when historical
  symbols are on); maps: `name_hist_n.png` / `name_hist_e.png` if present, else `name_n.png` / `name_e.png`.
* `pos` = top-left in world px **at camera (0, 0)** (same convention as layers).
* `layer` (optional): name of a layer → the decal becomes a child of that layer (inherits its parallax; `z` is
  relative to the layer, default 0 = drawn above it). `"playfield"` → y-sorted in the Playfield with sort key
  `base_y` (default `pos.y + height`). Absent / unknown → own holder with `parallax` ([x, y] or a number,
  default [1, 1]) and absolute `z` (default 0).
* `lit` (default true; unlit decals ignore 2D lights), `normal` (default true, uses the `_n` map if present),
  `emission` (+ `emission_strength`), `alpha`, `flip_h`, `frames`/`fps` (horizontal strip animation).
* `LayerStack.decals_by_name[name]` gives the node (e.g. for a story script that hides a banner).

### Measurements (A1 Karsk, RTX 4090, 1920×1080 window unless noted; `tests/visual/camera_motion.gd`)
Per-frame camera displacement in 1080p screen px (= canvas px; 4K rows in canvas px, 0.5 = 1 physical px).
"holds" = frames where the continuous camera moved but the picture did not. Image-measured scroll (strips)
matched the camera data in every frame of every phase (0 mismatches of 3 099 frames).

| run | walk: scroll steps | walk: holds while moving | player screen reversals (walk / run / walk back) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **before** (round-1 renderer, 144 Hz) | 0:380, 3:195 | 380 / 575 | 174 / 139 / 128 |
| after, 60 Hz | 3:113, 2:77, 4:21, 0:18, 1:11 | 7 / 229 | 1 / 0 / 1 |
| after, 144 Hz | 1:430, 2:79, 0:66 | 36 / 545 | 1 / 1 / 1 |
| after, 240 Hz | 1:588, 0:371 | 315 / 903 (< 1 px/frame at 1080p) | 1 / 1 / 1 |
| after, 240 Hz, 3840×2160 (k = 6) | 0.5:553, 1:311, 0:95 | 40 / 903 | 1 / 1 / 1 |
| pixel-locked 642×362 path, 240 Hz (same follow model, for comparison) | 0:763, 3:196 | 707 / 903 | 243 / 199 / 167 |

Player world reversals (feet sliding back): 0 in every run. Max |drawn − continuous| camera: 1.5 px before →
0.5 px (1080p) / 0.25 px (4K) after. Camera relative to the player at the end of each phase differs by
≤ 0.7 px between 60/144/240 Hz (frame-rate independence). Settling after a stop: monotonic (0 direction flips).
Hall lens shift (`camera_hall_rig_check.gd`, 144 Hz): before 86 steps of 3 px + 333 holds while moving → after
214 × 1 px + 22 × 2 px, 183 holds (sub-pixel tilt).
FPS (vsync off, walking): A1 1080p 1 305 avg / 633 1 %-low; A1 2160p 762 / 488; M1 1080p 1 324 / 552;
M1 2160p 596 / 335 (requirement ≥ 120).

## Asset manifest part — env_karsk (scene A1 "Verladebahnhof Karsk")

* **Date:** 2026-11-04 (round 2: detail, life, exploration objects, insignia switch)
* **Area owner:** env_karsk / karsk-env (environment art + ambient actors for `L_A1_Railyard`)
* **Generators (all deterministic, no network, original work):**
  * Art: `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/env_karsk.py` (`--no-preview` skips the review renders, `--docs` also rebuilds
    the inspect documents of round 1). Modules: `env_karsk.py` (orchestrator: layers, props, `_hist` twins,
    actor sprites, scene.json, locale), `env_karsk_lib.py` (palette access, pixel fonts, figures,
    shading helpers), `env_karsk_bg.py` (sky + parallax planes), `env_karsk_yard.py` (back-line buildings, ground,
    props, foreground frame), `env_karsk_life.py` (round-2 surfaces, exploration objects), `env_karsk_emblems.py`
    (hand-pixelled emblems), `env_karsk_sprites.py` (actor frame strips), `env_karsk_fx.py` (animated strips),
    `env_karsk_docs.py` (inspect-UI documents, round 1), `env_karsk_preview.py` (review composites).
    Built on `tools/pixelgen/pg.py` (unchanged).
  * Sound: `py -3 -P tools/audiogen/karsk_env_sfx.py` (uses `dsp.py`, `instruments.py`, `foley.py` read-only).
* **Determinism:** fixed integer seeds / `zlib.crc32` of names, never `hash()`. Verified 2026-11-04: SHA-1 of all
  art outputs (PNGs + scene.json) and all 9 WAVs identical after regeneration.
* **Licence / provenance (every file below):** original procedural pixel art and synthesised audio created for
  ASHES OF EMPIRES: THE LAST WINTER by the env_karsk generators (AI-authored code, project-owned, same licence as
  the game). No third-party images, fonts, textures, samples or recordings. The reference images in
  `documentation/references/` were never sampled, traced or embedded.
  Palette: `karsk` from `pg.PALETTES` + the shared C-17 red ramp `pg.PALETTES['sepia']['red']` (C-17 stamp,
  signal lamp, crane warning light, a scarf, the mitten, the cache cloth, medallion/poster red). Palette check on
  every colour PNG (layers, props, actors, fx): **OK**, no off-palette pixel.
* **Fictional content:** town "Karsk", slogan "ВОССТАНОВИМ ВМЕСТЕ" ("we rebuild together"), signs КАРСК / ЧАЙ /
  КВАС / КОНТОРА / ДЕПО / СКЛАД 2, stencils, numbers. The teaching board (УЧЕБНЫЙ) is a disconnected fictional
  training device.
* **Insignia (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §12.4):** the base files show a *fictional gear-and-wheat emblem*; the
  `*_hist.png` twins (identical except the emblem pixels) show the real Soviet **hammer & sickle**, hand-pixelled
  at 11/9/7/5 px (`env_karsk_emblems.py`). Placements — all in-world iconography of the Soviet state inside the
  critical narrative, never UI/reward: both ends of the civic banner, the medallion of the КОНТОРА sign, the red
  header of the reconstruction poster, an old weathered stencil on the C-17 wagon door, the masthead of the
  newspaper on the bench.

### Insignia switch
Only the three files above carry an emblem; their `*_hist.png` twins (with `_hist_n` / `_hist_e` maps) are picked by
`LayerStack` through `Symbols.pick` when "historical_symbols" is on (applied at scene load, like every other
level). No decals are needed. Verified with in-engine captures at `--symbols=0` and `--symbols=1`.

### Actor sprites (`actors/`) and actor scenes (`scenes/levels/karsk/*.tscn`, scripts `scripts/levels/karsk/*.gd`)
All actors share `karsk_actor.gd`: background actors use LayerStack's parallax grid rule (locked to their layer in
the high-resolution world path) and every opaque sprite gets render-camera's texel material (`texel_light.gd`).
| Sprite strip(s) | Actor scene | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| `crows.png` + `_n` (11 × 14×12: idle, breathe, look, caw, preen, hop, take-off, 3 wing frames, glide) | `KarskCrows` | Four hooded crows on the PA cable: idle acting; nervous within 80 px (turn, hop, caw); fly off one by one within 34 px or when the player runs; return after 22–40 s (exp_crows). |
| `cat.png` + `_n` + `_e` (7 × 18×11) | `KarskCat` | Ginger cat curled by the barrel: breathing; lifts its head (fire-lit eyes) within 46 px, blinks / ear / tail; settles again (exp_cat). |
| `train_loco.png` + `_e`, `train_wag_{box,tank,flat,gond}.png`, `train_puff.png` (8 × 18²) | `KarskTrain` | Goods train (loco + tender + 6–9 wagons) on the mid-plane embankment (parallax 0.45) every ~40–50 s, alternating direction, lingering smoke plume; first one enters the view ~4 s after arrival (exp_distant_train). |
| `laundry_{sheet,shirt,towel,johns,apron,cloth}.png` + `_n` (4 sway frames each) | `KarskLaundry` | Barrack laundry (parallax 0.72): gentle phase-shifted sway, flutters in gusts. |
| `window_signalman.png` (7 × 24²) | `KarskWindowLife` (cabin) | The signalman's silhouette in the amber window: sits, drinks tea, crosses, bends over the desk. |
| `window_near_{0..3}.png` (4 × 7×11 / 9×11) | `KarskWindowLife` (small, parallax 0.72) | Somebody at a lit background window now and then, or a curtain drawn. |
| `glint.png` + `_e` (5 × 5²), `cache_cloth.png` (4 × 7×5), `mitten.png` + `_n` (4 × 9×10) | `KarskGlint` | Stash glint every 4–7.5 s (exp_stash); red cloth corner under the stairs flutters only in gusts (exp_stairs_under); the lost mitten on the fence post leans in gusts (exp_mitten). |
| `lamp_bulb.png` + `_e` | `KarskLamp` | Tired platform lamp 2 with its own light: stutters every 6–15 s, sometimes drops out ~1 s (≤ 3 transitions/s; reduced motion: dips ≥ 70 %). |
| `breath_puff.png` (6 × 6²), `fx/barrel_flames.png` + `_e` (**8 × 18×20, redrawn as one merged licking fire**) | `KarskBarrel`, `KarskBreath` | Flames + sparks (more in gusts) + the workers' breath, y-sorted with the barrel; breath of the family and the tea vendor. |
| — | `KarskWind` | Gusts every 9–20 s: spindrift along the ground and off the roof edges, shared `gust` value for laundry / mitten / cloth / sparks. |
| new fx strip `fx/samovar_steam.png` (6 × 14×26) | scene.json fx | Steam over the samovar in the tea-stall hatch. |

## Asset manifest part — karsk_skyline (background buildings of scene A1 "Verladebahnhof Karsk")

* **Date:** 2026-11-04
* **Area owner:** karsk-skyline (redesign of the background planes of `L_A1_Railyard` after the user feedback
  "bei der ersten Szene sehe ich hinten so komisch aussehende Gebäude … realistisch oder typisch russisch").
* **Generator:** `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/env_karsk_skyline.py` (add `--no-preview` to skip the review composites).
  Modules: `env_karsk_skyline.py` (orchestrator + mid plane, manifests, palette check, previews),
  `env_karsk_skyline_lib.py` (plane geometry, depth scale, palette access, haze chains, fonts incl. bold rooftop
  capitals, emblem masks, QA), `env_karsk_skyline_kit.py` (shapes, windows, Khrushchyovka, grain elevator, rooftop
  slogan, chimneys, masts, pylons, birches, people), `env_karsk_skyline_arch.py` (Orthodox church, Stalinist
  administration, tower crane, izba, garages), `env_karsk_skyline_far.py` (far_industry, far_city),
  `env_karsk_skyline_near.py` (near_bg), `env_karsk_skyline_preview.py` (review composites, sun check).
  Depends only on `tools/pixelgen/pg.py` (unchanged) — independent of the `env_karsk_*` railyard generator.
* **Determinism:** fixed seeds, no network. Verified: SHA-1 of all 17 outputs identical after regeneration.
* **Licence / provenance:** original procedural pixel art created for ASHES OF EMPIRES: THE LAST WINTER by the
  karsk-skyline generator (AI-authored code, project-owned, same licence as the game). No third-party images,
  fonts or textures; no photo was traced or sampled (architecture drawn from general knowledge of Soviet
  provincial towns around 1964). Palette `karsk` + the shared C-17 red ramp (`pg.PALETTES['sepia']['red']`) only;
  palette check of every colour / emission PNG passes (no off-palette pixels).
* **Fictional / historical content:** the slogan "СЛАВА ТРУДУ!" ("Glory to labour", generic civic slogan) stands
  in red sheet-metal letters on the elevator's silo gallery. §12.4: `mid.png` shows the fictional gear-and-wheat
  emblem beside it (red gear, golden ear), `mid_hist.png` the real hammer & sickle — identical files except the
  11×11 emblem box (77 px). Other text: СКЛАД (church used as warehouse), ДЕПО, year "1953" on the Stalinist
  attic. The red star on the spire is generic Soviet imagery (both variants).

### Files (`assets/environments/karsk_skyline/`)
All lit planes ship `<name>_n.png` (normal map, OpenGL +Y up) and `<name>_e.png` (emission: lit windows, red
obstruction / crane lamps, gilt spire edge is *not* emissive). Sizes, `pos`, parallax and z are **identical** to the
railyard layers they replace.

| File | Size | pos / parallax / z | Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| `far_city.png` + `_n` + `_e` | 802×112 | [0,118] / 0.12 / -90 | Town on the horizon (1.27 px/m): panel-block districts in two hazy rows with tiny cranes, old quarter with a little church, bread factory stacks, power station with **two hyperboloid cooling towers at x 546 / 662 flanking the sun** (tops 149 / 157 = unchanged tower_plume anchors), boiler house, two striped chimneys (smoke anchor 688,123 unchanged), switchyard + pylon lines, guyed TV mast with red lamps near the sun (x 757). |
| `far_industry.png` + `_n` + `_e` | 968×147 | [0,83] / 0.25 / -84 | Industrial belt (2.64 px/m): thermal power station (boiler house, turbine hall, coal conveyor, two red/white striped chimneys at x 36 / 50), brick saw-tooth halls with arched windows, Intze water tower, telescopic gasholder, a microdistrict of five-storey panel blocks with antennas, a brick school, low warehouses, east factory chimney (x 872), pylons; bypass railway embankment with rails at y 216. |
| `mid.png` + `_n` + `_e`, `mid_hist.png` + `_hist_n` + `_hist_e` | 1224×185 | [0,49] / 0.45 / -74 | The readable town (4.75 px/m): **grain elevator** (x 76–304: 5 silos, patched shell hole on silo 2, gallery with the rooftop slogan + emblem, working house, conveyor bridge, brick receiving building, a worker with a ladder), **five-domed church turned warehouse** (x 336–452: crosses removed, rusty dome with missing sheets, boarded windows, scaffolding, СКЛАД), **Khrushchyovka** (x 467–591: 2 sections, 5 storeys, panel joints, balconies with laundry / firewood, stairwell windows, entrance canopies, antennas), garages, **Stalinist railway administration** (x 640–790: rusticated ground floor, portico, balustrades, clock tower, colonnaded belvedere, gilt spire with red star, "1953", 3 lit windows), izba, **panel block under construction + tower crane** (x 850–1030, mast at 1000, panel hoisted high, red jib lamp, two erectors), log houses, district boiler house with striped chimney (x 1150), garages, birches; **far railway embankment** across the plane (rail heads y 209, sleepers y 210 = KarskTrain rail). |
| `near_bg.png` + `_n` + `_e` | 1570×113 | [0,137] / 0.72 / -60 | Near quarter (7.6 px/m): two-storey **timber barrack** (x 4–160, carved window frames, stovepipe through a window, laundry line to the pole at x 200), **izby** with steep snowy roofs and carved nalichniki (x 208, 412), birches, woodsheds and log piles, brick **locomotive shed ДЕПО** (x 690–1000, arched gates, row of smoke jacks), low goods shed, **half-stone merchant house** (x 1150–1270), garages, fences. |
| `skyline.json` | — | — | Machine-readable integration data: the 4 layer entries, the 12 fx entries, laundry pegs, hotspot marks. |

Depth scale (consistent, ∝ parallax as for a pinhole camera; the playfield person is hero-scale 47 px):
architecture px/m = 10.56 × parallax → near_bg 7.6 (person 13 px, storey 21 px), mid 4.75 (person 8 px, storey
13 px), far_industry 2.64 (storey 7 px), far_city 1.27 (storey 3 px). Haze: every plane is painted with its own
material ramps (far: sky ramp, mid: sage/stone/wood, near: teal/wood/brick) and a ground-fog pass that shifts colours
along palette chains toward the air light near the plane base (ordered dither, no new colours).

Composition rules applied: the sun disc (screen 612,190) is kept free by low buildings on its path in every plane at
cam 0 / 640 / 1280 (visible fraction of the disc 0.84 / 0.51 / 0.33, before 0.74 / 0.13 / 0.04); nothing busy sits on
the roofline of the signal box (cam 0–186: the Khrushchyovka hides fully behind it at cam 0, the church dome stays
below its roof at cam 186), the tea stall or the office (cam 1280: construction site stays below the office roof, the
crane's panel hangs high, the boiler chimney moved east of the office); no tall mast behind the tea stall.

<a id="part-explore_karsk"></a>

## Asset manifest part — explore_karsk (exploration content of the Karsk railyard, Alexei's chapter)

All files below are **original works** made for this project. The close-ups are generated procedurally by
`tools/pixelgen/explore_karsk.py` on top of the shared, read-only `tools/pixelgen/pg.py` (palettes, Bayer dither,
value noise, `Layer`). The small Cyrillic bitmap fonts (5×7, 3×5), the hammer-and-sickle masks and the fictional
gear-and-wheat emblem are defined in that module. No pixels were copied, traced or sampled from
`documentation/references/` or any other source; no third-party images, fonts or textures are embedded. All German
text was written for this project.

* **Date:** 2026-11-04 · **Area owner:** karsk-explore
* **Licence / rights:** project-owned original work (AI-authored code and text), same licence as the game.
* **Provenance:** deterministic generator, seeds = crc32 of the image/part names:
  `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/explore_karsk.py` (writes `assets/explore/karsk/*.png`);
  `--preview` additionally writes ×3 review sheets to the folder in the environment variable
  `EXPLORE_KARSK_PREVIEW` (never into the project). Verified: two runs produce byte-identical files (SHA-1).
* **Format:** 16 files, 256×160 px RGBA (opaque), pixel art 1:1, shown ×3 by the DiscoveryView (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §12.1,
  limit 320×200). No normal/emission maps: the close-ups are UI images, not lit world sprites.
* **Palette:** `karsk` ramps of `pg.PALETTES` (night, teal, sage, sky, stone, rust, copper, snow, wood) plus the
  shared C-17 red ramp `pg.PALETTES['sepia']['red']` (banner cloth, mitten, rag, ink). The generator aborts on any
  off-palette pixel. 25–40 colours per image, ordered dither only along band edges.
* **Light:** follows the scene's key lights (ART_DIRECTION §5): low dawn from the back right (warm rims on right
  edges), cold sky ambient, local warm sources (fire barrel, office lamp, signal-box window).
* **Insignia (§12.4):** `newspaper.png` / `banner.png` carry the fictional gear-and-wheat emblem; their twins
  `newspaper_hist.png` / `banner_hist.png` show the hammer and sickle (real Soviet state insignia, same layout as the
  Karsk environment's emblems). Twins differ only in the emblem pixels (banner: the two end emblems, rows 41–76;
  newspaper: the masthead emblem). Loaders resolve the path through `Symbols.pick()` (setting `historical_symbols`,
  CLI `--symbols=0|1`); `Explore.entry_image()` and the DiscoveryView already do. Shown only as in-world iconography
  of the Soviet state inside the critical narrative — never in UI chrome, rewards or as player decoration.
* **Content:** all in-world texts stay inside the fictional 1964 and Alexei's voice; the eight real-history cards
  (`hist.soviet.*`, §12.3) are written by the history specialists and only referenced by id.

### Close-ups (`assets/explore/karsk/`)
| File | Find (entry id) | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `barrack.png` | `karsk.barrack` | Clapboard wall of the two-storey barrack: door, paler rectangle with four rusty screw holes where the "Sonderkontingent" sign hung, frosted window with a tin plate and a stovepipe (elbow, cap, smoke). |
| `cat.png` | `karsk.cat` | Ginger tabby "Pjatiletka" curled on a scrap of sacking beside the fire barrel (glowing holes, flames), head raised with half-open eyes; the workers' coats and felt boots at the frame edges; firelight on the snow. |
| `stairs_under.png` | `karsk.stairs_under` | Under the signal-box stair: treads seen from below, three loose bricks pulled from the foundation, opened oilcloth bundle with a red rag, pegged travel chess mid-game, score sheet. |
| `loudspeaker.png` | `karsk.loudspeaker` | Timber PA mast with crossarm, junction box, cables and two flared horn loudspeakers; snow, icicles, rust tears. |
| `newspaper.png` | `karsk.newspaper` | Page 3 of the fictional local paper "КАРСКАЯ ПРАВДА" on the bench slats: masthead with the gear-and-wheat emblem, page number, headline, the halftone "commission" photo (men in coats among trees), columns, the round "ДЕПО" library stamp, wet margin, folded corner. |
| `newspaper_hist.png` | `karsk.newspaper` | Same with the hammer and sickle in the masthead. |
| `wagon_chalk.png` | `karsk.wagon_chalk` | Oxide-red boards of wagon 24-311 with steel straps and rivets: chipped stencil, layers of chalk tallies, ticks, arrows, a circled 20, a crossed-out number, and at the bottom, almost washed away, "40 ЧЕЛ.". |
| `grain.png` | `karsk.grain` | The grain elevator: five concrete silos with a patched shell hole and rust tears, head house and gallery, loading chute into a hopper wagon stencilled "КОРИДОР 3", spilled grain and sparrows on the track bed. |
| `snowman.png` | `karsk.snowman` | Two-ball kids' snowman with a tin-can "ushanka" (bent tin ear flaps), coal eyes, a hex-bolt nose, nut buttons, one stick arm saluting; wooden sled beside, small footprints around. |
| `banner.png` | `karsk.banner` | Red cloth banner on the timber hoarding: fresh cream "ВОССТАНОВИМ ВМЕСТЕ" following the folds, gear-and-wheat emblems at both ends, the older slogan "СЛАВА ТОВ." shimmering through, the name after it painted out with darker brushwork; nails, snow on the hem. |
| `banner_hist.png` | `karsk.banner` | Same with the hammer and sickle at both ends. |
| `stash.png` | `karsk.stash` | Top-down: lifted slab, rusty tin box and its lid; inside a round-cut X-ray film with grooves, a ghostly ribcage and a burnt centre hole ("music on ribs"), white wax-pencil scrawl. |
| `kontora_shelf.png` | `karsk.kontora_shelf` | Freight-office shelf of year binders 1934–1948 with the 1939 binder missing: dust outline, darker shadow, the "1939" label still glued to the shelf edge; stamp, ink pad, boxes; warm lamp light. |
| `crows.png` | `karsk.crows` | Three hooded crows (grey mantles, black hoods and wings) on the snowy PA cable next to the timber mast; one looks straight at the viewer, one preens; far yard silhouettes in the haze. |
| `cooling_towers.png` | `karsk.cooling_towers` | Two hyperboloid cooling towers in the dawn haze with drifting steam, a chimney, a power line and a barbed-wire fence in the snowy foreground. |
| `mitten.png` | `karsk.mitten` | A child's red knitted mitten (pale snowflake band, ribbed cuff, thumb) pushed onto a concrete fence post, snow cap, chain-link fence and yard silhouettes behind. |

## Asset manifest — optional mini-games (area: minigames)

Owner: minigames specialist · Date: 2026-11-05 · Engine: Godot 4.7.2 · Contract: TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §12.8
(user request: "mehr kleine mini games hinzufügen").

Everything listed here is **original work**, generated procedurally by project scripts on 2026-11-05. No pixel,
sample or melody was taken from `documentation/references/` or any external source; no downloads, no AI media.
Colours come from `data/palettes.json` (`karsk` snow/stone/wood/rust/teal/night/copper, `archive`
wood/amber/paper/ink/skin ramps). The porter's face in "Das Radio" is the existing portrait
`assets/characters/porter/portrait.png` (characters area), only drawn, not modified. No regime insignia anywhere.
Licence: original project work (same terms as the rest of the game).

### The three mini-games

| Explore entry | Scene / script | Place (hotspot, world px) | Game |
|---|---|---|---|
| `karsk.shunting` (detail) | `scenes/ui/minigames/Shunting.tscn` · `shunting_game.gd` + `shunting_logic.gd` | Karsk `exp_shunting` [1112, 256], beside the dwarf shunting signal | Inglenook-style shunting from above: headshunt (loco + 2 wagons), points 1/2, sidings 1 (3), 2 (2), 3 (2); four wagons (tank, coal, panels, covered). Build the evening train on siding 1 as the waybill says (points→buffer: panels, coal, tank). Optimal 6 trips (BFS solver in the logic, also feeds the third hint level). |
| `karsk.chess` (detail) | `ChessProblem.tscn` · `chess_game.gd` + `chess_logic.gd` | Karsk `exp_chess` [362, 256], at the sleeper stack left of the signal-box stairs (next to `exp_stairs_under`) | The day/night-shift peg chess on a crate lid: mate in two for the day shift (White Kg1 Qc4 Nh6 Pf2 g2 h2 vs Black Kh8 Qb2 Ra8 Re8 Pa7 b7 g7 h7): 1. Dg8+ Txg8 (forced) 2. Sf7# (smothered mate). The line is hard-coded; every other move is refused with a reason (illegal, night-shift piece, too slow: Db1+, Sf7+ Kg8, Sxg8 …). |
| `berlin.radio_dial` (detail) | `RadioDial.tscn` · `radio_game.gd` + `radio_logic.gd` | Berlin `exp_radio_dial` [158, 300], left end of the porter's counter (28 px from `exp_porter`) | Tune the 1955 set from the Reichssender (red line) to the pencil dot: static thins, magic eye closes, a jazz trio comes through; listen 5 s while the porter reads. When his paper sinks, the needle must be back on the red line (station key R); being "caught" only resets the needle and costs 40 % of the listening. |

Shared base `scripts/ui/minigames/minigame_base.gd` (extends `ui_modal.gd`): chrome (kicker, title, subtitle,
instruction, status, hint plate, buttons, key hints), UIRoot modal stack or stand-alone input, `finished(success)`,
`open(ctx)`, auto mode (success after 0.2 s), hint after 2 refusals, solved hold 2.4 s (confirm continues),
`demo_solve()` = synthetic key events only (`Input.parse_input_event`, keycode + physical_keycode). Keyboard,
mouse and pad (d-pad/stick, A, B, Y/X). All text via Loc: `data/locale/de_minigames.json` (`mg.*`); find texts in
`de_explore_karsk.json` / `de_explore_berlin.json` (protagonist voice).

### Generators

* `tools/pixelgen/gen_minigames.py` — `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/gen_minigames.py`, then import (shared lock).
  Deterministic (seeds = `crc32` of names). Drawn 1:1, **stored x3 nearest** in `assets/ui/minigames/`.
* `tools/audiogen/minigame_sfx.py` — `py -3 -P tools/audiogen/minigame_sfx.py` (numpy only, deterministic).
  44.1 kHz / 16-bit mono WAV in `assets/audio/sfx/`; loops carry a RIFF `smpl` chunk (import loop_mode detect).

<a id="part-minigames_r7_kb"></a>

## Asset manifest — round-7 mini-games „Der Morsetaster“ and „Rohrpost“ (area: minigames_r7_kb)

Owner: minigames_r7_kb specialist · Date: 2026-11-06 · Engine: Godot 4.7.2 · Contract: TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §12.1/§12.8
(user request this round: more play time, new mini-game ideas).

Everything listed here is **original work**, generated procedurally by the project scripts below on 2026-11-06. No
pixel, sample or melody was taken from `documentation/references/` or any other source; no downloads, no AI media,
no recordings. Colours come from `data/palettes.json` (`karsk` night/teal/sage/sky/stone/rust/copper/snow/wood,
`archive` paper/ink/blue/amber/wood/night, `berlin` patina/snow) plus a polished-brass ramp and a violet stamp ink
defined in the generators. The 5×7 / 3×5 pixel fonts (Latin capitals with umlauts, the full Russian alphabet, digits)
are hand-made bitmaps in `tools/pixelgen/mg_r7_kb_lib.py`. No regime insignia, no flags, no emblems anywhere (§12.4):
the Karsk desk shows a plain tea-glass holder, the archive board a plain brass plate.
Historical model: the Morse registers (paper-tape inkers), sounders and keys of Soviet railway telegraphy, Russian
Morse code with its international equivalents as printed on wall charts; the hand-switched house pneumatic-tube
systems of large German offices. Licence: original project work (same terms as the rest of the game).

### The two mini-games

| Explore entry | Scene / scripts | Place (hotspot, world px) | Game |
|---|---|---|---|
| `karsk.morse` (detail, area `karsk`) | `scenes/ui/minigames/MorseKey.tscn` · `scripts/ui/minigames/morse_game.gd` + `morse_logic.gd` | L_A1_Railyard `exp_morse` [590, 256], right corner of the signal box where the telegraph line comes in (38 px from the side task's `exp_task_signalbox`) | The duty officer has gone for tea; the telegraph rings. The register prints the dispatcher's telegram on its tape, the sounder clicks, the call lamp flashes. Alexei takes the routine (ВАГ 24311) down by ear; the player decodes НЕ ВЗВЕШИВАТЬ („nicht verwiegen“) letter by letter with the wall chart АЗБУКА МОРЗЕ (7×6: Russian Morse with the international equivalents), then keys the receipt ПР (= принял) on the key: short = dot, held past the mark = dash, pauses never matter, a wrong element restarts only that letter. The line answers Р. Telegram line already on the form: П 2417 ОПОЗД 40 МИН (delay). |
| `berlin.rohrpost` (detail, area `berlin`) | `scenes/ui/minigames/Rohrpost.tscn` · `scripts/ui/minigames/rohrpost_game.gd` + `rohrpost_logic.gd` | L_M1_Archive `exp_rohrpost` [932, 300], at the information desk of the reading room where the copper tube comes down from the ceiling | The house tube system's switch board (1938). Three capsules from the porter's lodge to the reading room, three boards of rising difficulty (2, 3, 4 knobs to turn; board 2 with a frozen main line, board 3 with a crossing, a return line and a knob that is not on the way). The capsule rushes through the brass tubes (whoosh); a wrong setting sends it to the registry, Abt. III, the director's office, the canteen, the plan archive or back to the lodge (each comes back with a small line), or jams it in a knob / the frozen line. Each delivered capsule shows what it brought: the porter's loan slip for a book about pigeons, the house management asking who turned up the heating, a sandwich for the librarian. |

Contract (§12.8, shared base `minigame_base.gd`): `open(ctx)`, `ctx.auto` resolves after 0.2 s, `finished(success)`
exactly once, Esc / „Aufgeben“ without penalty (nothing is ever lost), hints after two refusals — following the
Settings `puzzle_hints` (0 never, 2 at once) and the current letter / board (Morse: the group's length, then the field
glows; receipt: how to key, then the rhythm of П and Р; Rohrpost: follow the tube back from the reading room, then
„Weiche n steht noch falsch“ with the knob ringed), `demo_solve()` plays through synthetic key events only (~25 s each).
Keys — Morse: arrows/WASD/d-pad on the chart, Enter/Space/E/pad A write, R/pad Y repeats the letter; receipt: hold
Space/Enter/E/pad A or the mouse on the key, R plays the receipt once. Rohrpost: arrows choose a knob (every knob
reachable), Space/E/pad A turn, Enter/pad X/Y call the capsule, R puts the board's knobs back; mouse on knobs (right
click turns back) and the button. Layout: one custom-drawn stage per game that fits the panel — pixel art ×3, or ×2
(×1 as a last resort) when the panel is short (large text, hint plate); room for the hint is reserved so nothing
jumps. Text scale: all drawn texts follow it; the Morse form moves up over the register and stacks its translations
before any text shrinks, the Rohrpost side column switches to a compact list instead of shrinking below 100 %; board
labels never go below their normal size. High contrast: white paper, black ink, yellow cursors and rings.
All text: `data/locale/de_minigames_r7_kb.json` (`mg.morse.*`, `mg.tube.*`, find texts `explore.karsk.morse.*`,
`explore.berlin.rohrpost.*`); entries: `data/explore/minigames_r7_kb.json`.

### Generators

* `tools/pixelgen/gen_mg_morse.py` — `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/gen_mg_morse.py`, then import (shared lock).
* `tools/pixelgen/gen_mg_rohrpost.py` — `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/gen_mg_rohrpost.py`, then import. Holds the three
  boards and verifies them before drawing (solvable, start not solved, a unique fewest-change setting with 2/3/4
  changes); writes `rohrpost_layout.json`, the single source of the puzzle for `rohrpost_logic.gd`.
* `tools/pixelgen/mg_r7_kb_lib.py` — shared canvas helpers and the pixel fonts of both generators.
* `tools/audiogen/mg_audio_r7_kb.py` — `py -3 -P tools/audiogen/mg_audio_r7_kb.py` (numpy only). 44.1 kHz / 16-bit
  mono WAV; loops are built periodic and carry a RIFF `smpl` chunk (import loop_mode detect).
All deterministic (seeds = `crc32` of names): a re-run reproduces all 28 files byte for byte (checked 2026-11-06).
UI art is drawn 1:1 and **stored ×3 nearest**; the two find close-ups are stored 1:1 (the DiscoveryView shows them ×3).

### Files

|---|---|---|

### Verification (2026-11-06)

* `tests/test_minigames_r7_kb.gd` (17 cases, ~1340 checks): chart complete and unique with correct international
  equivalents; telegram timeline (bell, one click/release per element, every letter arrives once, slower target words);
  decoding and receipt accepted, refusals with reasons (too early, wrong letter, wrong element restarts the letter);
  boards solvable with unique 2/3/4-change solutions matching the generator, knob-by-knob progress through the wrong
  offices, jam / frozen / loop detection, continuous capsule path; both scenes: §12.8 contract, auto mode, keyboard play
  to the solution through `modal_input`, every knob reachable by arrows, hint after two refusals, `puzzle_hints` 0/1/2,
  Esc without a find, `finished` exactly once, `demo_solve` sends synthetic keys only; data, Loc keys (no overrides,
  calm German typography), art sizes, layout files, audio format and loop chunks, placement (content-owned hotspots
  inside the walk band, clear of story spots / finds / other mini-games, one Interactable each under Playfield).
* `tests/visual/minigames_r7_kb_shots.tscn` (windowed, real Game path): boots Main into the level, puts the
  protagonist on the find, captures the prompt, opens the find through `Game.interact()`, refuses twice by keys (hint),
  runs `demo_solve()` while capturing every stage, checks `finished(true)`, the DiscoveryView and the recorded find.

## Asset manifest part -- env_berlin (M1 Berliner Bauarchiv round 2, plan documents)

All files below are **original works generated procedurally** by the scripts in `tools/pixelgen/`
(`env_berlin.py` drives `env_berlin_room.py`, `env_berlin_hall.py`, `env_berlin_library.py`,
`env_berlin_ext.py`, `env_berlin_props.py`, `env_berlin_fg.py`, `env_berlin_fx.py`, `env_berlin_actors.py`,
`env_berlin_insignia.py`, `env_berlin_docs.py`, helpers `env_berlin_kit.py`, `env_berlin_layout.py` and the
shared, read-only `pg.py`).  No pixels were copied, traced or sampled from the reference images in
`documentation/references/` (style study only).  No third-party assets, fonts or textures are embedded;
the tiny bitmap fonts are hand-defined in `env_berlin_kit.py`.  Sounds: `tools/audiogen/berlin_env_sfx.py`
(numpy synthesis, no samples).

* Licence / rights: project-owned original artwork (generated for this project), freely
  redistributable with the game.  Provenance: deterministic generator, re-run with
  `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/env_berlin.py` and `py -3 -P tools/audiogen/berlin_env_sfx.py`.
* Date: 2026-11-04.  Palettes: `archive` (+ local `green`, `graphite` ramps), `berlin` (+ archive ink reds for flag
  cloth), `sepia`.
* Insignia (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE 12.4): the layers carry only the neutral art (fictional Reichssiegel /
  a plain eagle).  The historical variants (`*_hist.png`: Reichsadler with the wreathed swastika on the
  plaque and the AUFBAU poster, the swastika flag on the facade banners, eagle seals on plan A / plan B /
  Emil's slip) are separate files chosen at runtime by `Symbols.pick()` -- in-world iconography of the regime
  inside the critical narrative only, never UI, rewards or player decoration.  Drawn from original geometry
  in `env_berlin_insignia.py` (no traced emblem).
* The Great Hall is fictional and shown muted, not heroic.
* Locale `data/locale/de_env_berlin.json` is hand-written original German text (Konrad's voice).
* `env_berlin_stills.py` / `x1_*.png` were made by this area in round 1; they now belong to the transition
  work and are not rebuilt by `env_berlin.py` (see that owner's manifest part).

---
<a id="part-explore_berlin"></a>

## Asset manifest part -- explore_berlin (exploration content of the Berliner Bauarchiv, Konrad's chapter)

All files below are **original works** made for this project.  Close-ups are generated procedurally by
`tools/pixelgen/explore_berlin.py` on top of the shared, read-only `pg.py` (palettes, Bayer dither, noise); the
tiny bitmap fonts, the fictional Reichssiegel, the plain eagle and the swastika bitmaps are hand-defined in
that module.  No pixels were copied, traced or sampled from `documentation/references/` or any other source;
no third-party assets, fonts or textures are embedded.  All German text was written for this project.

* Licence / rights: project-owned original work, freely redistributable with the game.
* Provenance: deterministic generator (seeds = crc32 of the asset names; re-running produces byte-identical
  files): `py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/explore_berlin.py [--preview DIR] [names...]`.
* Date: 2026-11-04.  Size: 240 x 150 px each, pixel art 1:1, shown x3 by the DiscoveryView (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE
  12.1).  No normal/emission maps: close-ups are UI images, not lit world sprites.
* Palettes: `archive` (night, wall, wood, amber, paper, blue, ink), `berlin` (steel, snow, violet/night, stone,
  patina, glow), `sepia` (photo tones, red ramp), plus the two archive extras of the Berlin room
  (`green` lamp glass, `graphite` pencil).  The generator rejects any colour outside these ramps.
* Light follows the archive's key lights (ART_DIRECTION 5): cold daylight from the left, warm lamp accents.
* Insignia (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE 12.4): neutral file = fictional Reichssiegel (white obelisk in a black disc);
  `*_hist.png` = the real historical insignia, chosen at runtime by `Symbols.pick()` (setting
  `historical_symbols`, CLI `--symbols=0|1`).  Only in-world regime iconography inside the critical narrative,
  never UI, rewards or player decoration.  The two variants differ only in the insignia pixels.
* The real-history cards these finds unlock (`hist.reich.*`) are written by the history specialists and
  referenced by id only; in-world texts stay inside the fictional 1964.

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `assets/explore/berlin/master_plan.png` | general plan of the north-south axis behind glass: hall, axis, Spree, Tempelhof, pencil circle "Probekörper 1941", title block with the office stamp (neutral: Reichssiegel) |
| `assets/explore/berlin/master_plan_hist.png` | same, office stamp with eagle and swastika |
| `assets/explore/berlin/poster_aufbau.png` | the AUFBAU 1964 poster (same design as berlin-env's wall decal): dome as cream line drawing on night blue, Reichssiegel above |
| `assets/explore/berlin/poster_aufbau_hist.png` | same, eagle with the wreathed swastika above the dome |
| `assets/explore/berlin/notice.png` | cork board, typed list "Bestandsänderungen Achse Süd", every line "umgesetzt", ends at "Kolonnenstr. 12" |
| `assets/explore/berlin/pneumatic_tube.png` | pneumatic tube station, capsule in the basket, routing slip "zur Aussonderung – Eilt", a pencil heart "17 Uhr?" |
| `assets/explore/berlin/clock.png` | the pendulum regulator by the porter's lodge (brass pendulum, two weights) |
| `assets/explore/berlin/card_catalogue.png` | open catalogue drawer: guide cards C-15/C-16/C-18, the C-17 guide leaning into the gap, "entnommen s. Abt. III" |
| `assets/explore/berlin/reading_table.png` | reading table under a green lamp: request slip "Leser Nr. 114 – Ausweis eingezogen 2.11.", folded spectacles |
| `assets/explore/berlin/globe.png` | 1938 globe with inked borders, pasted-over countries and the red armistice line |
| `assets/explore/berlin/lib_shelf_1.png` | open chronicle 1942: "19. Januar" / "21. Januar", pages 41/42 run on |
| `assets/explore/berlin/lib_shelf_2.png` | row of red Kursbücher with the grey linen dummy "Sonderz. 42–44" standing proud |
| `assets/explore/berlin/lib_shelf_3.png` | atlas page: green settlement land with villages in a grid, grey "freizumachende Fläche", model farm 1:500 |
| `assets/explore/berlin/lib_shelf_4.png` | ward plan "Bettenhaus II, Umbau 1940", 80 beds, red-pencil cross and "ENTFÄLLT" |
| `assets/explore/berlin/lib_shelf_5.png` | Festschrift appendix "Herkunft der Werkstoffe" (Flossenbürg, Mauthausen, Groß-Rosen), photo with the cut-off worker |
| `assets/explore/berlin/lib_hidden.png` | the pushed-in back panel of shelf V, an envelope and Brandt's index card in block letters |
| `assets/explore/berlin/mouse.png` | mouse hole in the skirting board, sign "Zutritt nur für Befugte", "Bewohnerin: Akte", crumbs, thimble |
| `assets/explore/berlin/radio.png` | the porter's radio: lit dial on the line, pencil dot far right, green magic eye |
| `assets/explore/berlin/heater.png` | rib radiator under the reading-room window, two small red mittens drying |
| `assets/explore/berlin/pigeons.png` | five puffed pigeons on the snowy sill at dusk, the dome in the haze, frost on the pane |
| `assets/explore/berlin/photo.png` | the last frame of the department chronicle: group photo before the hall model, one face scratched to white, embossed caption |

## Asset manifest — characters (in-world sprite sheets + normal maps)

Area owner: characters specialist (`chars-sprites`) · Updated: **2026-11-04, round 2** · Engine target: Godot 4.7.2
(Dialogue portraits `portrait.png/json` are owned by the portrait generator `chargen_portrait*` and are not
listed here.)

> **Round 7 (2026-11-06):** Chapter-3 cast on this rig, `talk_front` / `talk_back`, grounded front / back walks,
> soft player turns -- see `characters_r7.md` (supersedes the row tables below where they differ).

### Provenance & licence (applies to every file below)
* **Original work, generated procedurally** by the project's own Python scripts (Pillow + numpy; no AI image
  models, no network, no third-party art). All heads, hats, hands, boots and props are hand-designed pixel
  maps written as ASCII in the generator source; bodies are rendered by the project's own paper-doll rig.
* The reference images in `documentation/references/` were not traced, sampled or embedded.
* Licence: project-owned original asset (same licence as the game's own content). No third-party attribution.
* Deterministic: re-running the generator reproduces byte-identical PNGs (no randomness anywhere).

### Reproduce
```bash
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters.py              # all 16 sheets + normal maps + review previews (~15 s)
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters.py vendor       # one character
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters.py --portraits  # additionally run the portrait generator (its owner's call)
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters_check.py        # validator (exit 1 on failure)
```

### Generator scripts
| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `tools/pixelgen/characters.py` | entry point: row layout per character, `sheet.json` (incl. idle-variant metadata, ground speed), previews |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_core.py` | G-buffer rig renderer: OKLab hue-shifted ramps, capsules / polygons / bitmaps, height-derived normals, shading (key, contact shadows, AO, inner seams, key-side edge seams, cool rim, selective outline with convex-corner anti-aliasing, lit-edge highlight, orphan-tone cleanup, deterministic fur / knit / felt / tweed textures) |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_heads.py` | **new:** hand-drawn heads for all 16 characters, side / front / back + blink / talk / glance-back variants |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_body.py` | humanoid paper-doll (side / front / back): orientation-aware hands (hanging, reaching, raised, open palm, fist; skin / glove / mitten), sleeve cuffs, coat fold relief, collars over the neck, IK that reaches the ground at full stride |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_anim.py` | keyframed poses: walk 8 (uniform planted-foot travel, weight shift, secondary-motion signals), idle 8 (two-stage breathing), blink, glance back, character gestures, work loops, talk 8 (two hand beats + nod), inspect 6, interact 5, front/back walk + idle |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_cast.py` | the 12 round-1 characters: materials, garments, props, hooks |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_cast_new.py` | **new:** librarian, porter, vendor, worker_b |
| `tools/pixelgen/characters_check.py` | contract validator (schema, rows per role, origin / ground contact, clipping, normal alpha / length, heights ±1 px, variant integrity, protagonist ground speed, orphan pixels) |

`tools/pixelgen/pg.py` is used unchanged (not modified).

### Sprite sheets — `assets/characters/<id>/` (cell 32×56, origin [16,54] = feet pixel, facing right)
Every sheet ships `sheet.png` (diffuse, RGBA, binary alpha), `sheet_n.png` (OpenGL +Y-up normal map, identical
alpha) and `sheet.json` (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §4). Optional extra keys: `ground_speed` {anim: px/s};
`anims[a].variants` + `variant_gap` (one-shot rows `sprite_rig.gd` mixes into a looping idle);
`anims[v].variant_of`.

Row sets: **protagonist** = idle_side, walk_side, idle_front, walk_front, idle_back, walk_back, inspect_side,
interact_side, talk_side (the 9 contract rows, unchanged order) + blink_side, look_side, gesture_side,
blink_front · **NPC** = idle_side, walk_side, idle_front, talk_side, blink_side, look_side, gesture_side,
blink_front (+ work_side where listed). Emil's order keeps idle_side, talk_side, idle_front first.

| id | height | rows | 1964 design (silhouette keys) | gesture / work |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `alexei` | 47 px | 13 | dark grey-brown fur ushanka, front flap up, ear-flaps loose (tie strings swing), padded dark-green coat (pocket flaps, dark buttons), grey knitted scarf, tan leather satchel on a cross strap, kirza boots with a patch | blows into his hands (breath puff) |
| `konrad` | 48 px | 13 | charcoal fedora with black band + snap brim, knee-long navy overcoat (lapels, half belt at the back, vent), ochre wool scarf with a swinging end, drafting tube on a strap, leather folder under the far arm, leather shoes | adjusts the hat brim |
| `irina` | 45 px | 8 | rust headscarf knotted under the chin, dark fringe, quilted slate dispatcher jacket with belt, rolled yellow signal flag at the back, dark skirt, felt boots with galoshes, clipboard (gestures with it while talking) | weight shift |
| `emil` | 44 px | 8 | bald crown, grey fringe, round spectacles (glint), grey moustache, open grey archive coat over a brown knit cardigan, shirt + tie, pencils in the breast pocket, stoop | pushes his glasses up |
| `worker_a` | 47 px | 9 | ushanka with flaps tied up, quilted olive telogreika with belt, padded trousers, felt boots with galoshes, canvas mittens, shovel on the shoulder | weight shift · work: warms hands |

| `berlin_child` | 31 px | 8 | navy beret, short coat, knee socks, toy bear | hugs the bear |
| `clerk` | 45 px | 8 | slicked side-parted hair, grey suit, white collar + tie, black sleeve protectors (Ärmelschoner), file folder | weight shift |

| `porter` **new** | 47 px | 9 | navy uniform cap with gold braid, white moustache, long double-breasted navy coat with brass buttons and a watch chain, black shoes | checks his watch · work: pocket watch |
| `vendor` **new** | 44 px | 9 | dark shawl with red roses (Pavlovo-Posad style), padded telogreika, grey work apron with a tea stain, skirt, felt boots; enamel teapot | blows into her hands · work: pours tea (stream) |

Heights are within ±1 px of round 1 (validator-enforced). Walk ground speed: Alexei 34.5 px/s, Konrad
35.0 px/s (player playback = 45 / ground_speed); civilians 15–25 px/s (NPC wander playback is scaled the same
way in `npc.gd`).

Animation: walk 8 frames @10 fps (contact & down frames 1 px lower, passing & up high; opposite arm swing with
lag; coat hem, scarf ends, satchel, ear-flap strings and scarf tails lag one frame), idle 8 frames @5 fps
(two-stage breathing, no blink), variants: blink (3 f), glance back over the shoulder (10 f), character
gesture (7–10 f), talk 8 frames @7 fps (rest → lift → open-palm beat with nod → settle → second beat →
rest, mouth open/close), inspect (lean in, hand to chin, blink in the hold), interact (anticipation → reach →
hold). Light is baked character-relative (warm key from the front-top of the facing direction, cool rim on
back edges, selective outline tinted to each material's darkest hue-shifted shade) so it stays valid when
mirrored; scene lights act through the normal maps (mirroring verified, see below).

### Insignia (TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §12.4)
Only the Reich building **guard** carries regime iconography, as in-world uniform detail of the coercive
apparatus (never on the protagonists or any player-side element): an armband on the near upper arm.
* `assets/characters/guard/sheet.png` (neutral, always present): grey-black band with the white pillar of the
  fictional "Reichssiegel".
* `assets/characters/guard/sheet_hist.png`: the historical red armband with white disc and black emblem (at 1x
  a 2-3 px detail). Same geometry and alpha as the neutral sheet; `sheet_n.png` and `sheet.json` serve both.
  Resolved at runtime by `Symbols.pick()` (via `LayerStack.canvas_tex` in `sprite_rig.gd`); verified in-engine
  with `--symbols=0` / `--symbols=1`.
No other character shows insignia (Soviet civilians and railway workers wear none in this slice).

### Localisation
`data/locale/de_characters.json` — `char.<id>.name`, `char.<id>.role` for all 16 ids (new: Bibliothekarin,
Pförtner, Teeverkäuferin, Gleisarbeiter) and `char.expression.<name>` (German).

### Runtime
`scripts/actors/sprite_rig.gd` (idle variants, `play_once`, soft `turn_to`), `scripts/actors/npc.gd`
(`idle_anim` override, soft turn toward the player, talk release on a resting frame, wander playback scaled by
ground speed). Tests: `tests/visual/CharLightTest.tscn` (windowed: normal-map mirroring under one lamp, both
facings), `tests/visual/CharRigSelftest.tscn` (headless logic test).

### Review images (not shipped; `_preview/` has `.gdignore`)
`assets/characters/_preview/`: `<id>_contact_3x.png`, `lineup_1x/3x/6x.png`, `lineup_front_6x.png`,
`lineup_silhouettes_3x.png`, `walk_strips_3x.png`, `walk_side_all.gif`, `<id>_walk_side_onion.png`,
`normal_relight_test.png` (incl. mirrored), `before_after_{side,front,walk}_4x.png`, `new_characters_4x.png`,
`guard_symbols_neutral_vs_hist_5x.png`.

## Asset manifest — dialogue portraits (round 2: living close-ups)

Area owner: chars-portraits specialist · Date: **2026-11-04** · Engine target: Godot 4.7.2

> **Round 7 (2026-11-06):** 17 speakers (the Chapter-3 seven are on this pipeline now), individual noses / chins /
> eyes / brows, Babushka's kerchief, cap clip fix -- see `portraits_r7.md`.

### Provenance & licence (applies to every file below)
* **Original work, generated procedurally** by the project's own Python scripts (numpy + Pillow; no AI image
  models, no network, no third-party art, no image inputs). Facial features (eyes, lids, brows, mouths,
  ears, wrinkles) are hand-designed pixel stamps / parametric pixel strokes written in the generator source.
* The reference images in `documentation/references/` were used **only** as internal lighting study
  (`user_ref_1`: navy body + single warm key). Nothing was traced, sampled, copied or embedded.
* Licence: project-owned original asset (same licence as the game's own content). No third-party attribution.
* Deterministic: re-running the generator reproduces identical PNGs (all noise / hashes are integer-seeded,
  never Python `hash()`).

### Reproduce
```bash
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py                    # all 9 speakers + previews (~7 min)
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py konrad irina       # selected speakers
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py --preview konrad   # previews only, no asset writes
## characters.py still calls chargen_portrait.main(); that module is now a shim that forwards here and
## skips speakers whose files are newer than the generator sources (--force rebuilds).
```

### Generator scripts
| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py` | entry point: scene assembly per speaker / expression / jaw state, render pipeline, PNG + JSON writer, previews |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2_sdf.py` | signed-distance sculpting nodes (ellipsoids, round cones, boxes, elliptic cylinders, smooth union / subtraction, warps, displacement, bounding) + orthographic sphere tracer (3×3 super-sampling, soft key shadow, occlusion) and material-majority downsampling |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2_anat.py` | parametric head after the planes of the head (cranium, forehead / mid-face blocks, cheekbones, malar pads, brow ridge, sockets + lids, nose bridge / tip / alae, muzzle, lips, rotatable jaw group, box chin, ears, neck), landmarks, torso |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2_paint.py` | key / fill / ambient / rim light model, per-material hue-shifted ramps, material-aware smoothing of the light field, posterisation, cluster cleanup, selective outline, depth-break lines, painted background |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2_face.py` | eye stamps (near / far eye × neutral, concerned, determined, surprised, closed), parametric brows and mouths (rest, half, open; teeth, tongue) on a tone-relative canvas |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2_cast.py` | hats (fedora, ushanka flaps down / up, peaked cap without insignia), headscarves (nape knot / chin knot, hem, clip volumes), hair (short, fringe, bun), garments (coats with lapels, quilting, roll-necks, scarves, shawl, apron, layered cardigan + shirt + tie, buttons, straps), texture passes (knit ribs, felt grain, fur tufts, stitches, hair strands, cloth folds, polka dots, printed scarf borders), stubble, wrinkles, eye bags, spectacles, pencils, gold tooth; the nine speaker specs |
| `tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait.py` | compatibility shim (old entry point, forwards to chargen_portrait2) |

### Files — `assets/characters/<id>/`
| File | Format |
|---|---|
| `portrait.png` | 448×112 strip, 4 frames of 112×112 = the REST frames in `expressions` order (contract of TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE §4, unchanged) |
| `portrait_anim.png` | 448×672 grid: **column = expression index** (same order as `expressions`), **row = state**: 0 `rest`, 1 `talk_half`, 2 `talk_open`, 3 `blink`, 4 `blink_talk_half`, 5 `blink_talk_open` (row = mouth + 3 × eyes_closed) |
| `portrait.json` | `{"size": [112,112], "expressions": [...], "anim": {"file", "layout", "rows", "talk_rows", "blink_offset": 3, "mouth_fps": 11, "blink_ms": 130, "blink_interval_s": [2.6, 5.2], "generator"}}` — readers that only know `size` + `expressions` keep working |

All frames are opaque (painted, scene-tinted background), 1 texel = 1 logical pixel, shown ×3 (nearest).

| id | look (bust, 3/4 view facing right, warm key front-right, cool rim back-left) | background |
|---|---|---|
| `alexei` | fur ushanka with hanging ear flaps + tie strings, grey ribbed roll-neck, quilted green padded coat, satchel strap, stubble, tired eyes | Karsk teal |
| `konrad` | felt fedora (tapered crown, pinch, ribbon band, snap brim), short dark hair, knitted sand scarf tucked into the dark-blue overcoat, lapels, drafting-tube strap | Berlin violet |
| `irina` | madder-red headscarf knotted at the nape (rolled hem, gathered folds, ochre printed border, hanging corner), auburn hair at the edge, mustard roll-neck, quilted slate dispatcher jacket | Karsk teal |
| `emil` | bald crown with grey fringe, round wire spectacles with glare, grey moustache, jowls, forehead lines, grey archive coat over a brown cardigan, shirt + green tie, pencils in the breast pocket, stoop | archive navy |
| `vendor` | off-white kerchief with a blue printed border, blush, teal quilted jacket, brown roll-neck, white apron bib; a gold tooth when she talks | Karsk teal |
| `worker_a` | ushanka with flaps tied up, heavy stubble, weathered lines, olive telogreika, dark roll-neck | Karsk teal |
| `babushka` | navy polka-dot headscarf knotted under the chin, deep wrinkles, grey knitted shawl, dark coat | Karsk teal |
| `librarian` | grey-streaked hair in a bun, half-moon reading glasses on a chain, high cream collar, bottle-green ribbed cardigan | archive navy |
| `porter` | doorman's peaked cap (cloth band, glossy visor, brass cord — **no badge, no insignia**), heavy jowls, navy uniform coat with brass buttons, shirt + dark tie | Berlin violet |

No regime insignia appear in any portrait (portraits are player-facing UI chrome, see TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE
§12.4), so no `_hist` variants are needed.

### Runtime (scripts/ui/dialogue_box.gd)
The DialogueBox reads the `anim` block: while the typewriter types, the mouth row follows the last revealed
letter (vowels open, labials / spaces / punctuation closed, other letters half; at most `mouth_fps` changes per
second; reduced motion halves the rate and never opens fully), rests during punctuation pauses and at the end
of the line; the eyes blink every `blink_interval_s` (sometimes twice) for `blink_ms`; the expression column
stays fixed per line. Without an `anim` block the strip stays static; without a portrait the silhouette
placeholder is shown.

### Verification harness
`tests/visual/portrait_harness.tscn` shows the real DialogueBox for all nine speakers × 4 expressions (+ talk
and blink poses) over in-engine level captures and checks: animation grid in use, mouth changes while typing,
mouth rests in punctuation pauses and at the end of the line, expression column constant per line, blink
closes and re-opens the eyes, 112×112 regions, silhouette placeholder for speakers without portrait, static
strips stay static, dock_top / toast suppression, choice guard. Never writes saves or settings.
```bash
godot --path . res://tests/visual/portrait_harness.tscn -- --out=<dir> [--bg-karsk=<png>] [--bg-berlin=<png>] [--only=id,id]
godot --headless --path . res://tests/visual/portrait_harness.tscn -- --out=<dir>     # checks only
```

### Review images (not shipped; `_preview/` has `.gdignore`)
`assets/characters/_preview/portraits_v2_<id>_3x.png` (4 expressions × 6 states per speaker),
`portraits_compare_old_new_2x.png` (old strips left, new right), `portraits_all_rest_2x.png` (= refreshed
`portraits_all_2x.png`), `portraits_states_2x.png`, `portraits_dialogue_ingame_all.png` (in-engine DialogueBox
panels, 9 speakers × neutral / concerned / determined / surprised / talk / blink) and single in-engine captures
`portraits_dialogue_{konrad,alexei,vendor_talk,placeholder,docktop_emil}.png`. The older
`<id>_portraits_3x.png` files show the first-generation portraits and are obsolete.

## Asset manifest — characters, round 7 (Chapter-3 cast finished, talk_front, soft turns, grounded front walks)

Area owner: characters specialist · Date: **2026-11-06, round 7** · Engine target: Godot 4.7.2
Extends `characters.md` (round 2, still valid for everything not listed here). Portraits: `portraits_r7.md`.

### Provenance & licence (applies to every file below)
* **Original work, generated procedurally** by the project's own Python scripts (Pillow + numpy; no AI image
  models, no network, no third-party art, no image inputs). Heads, hats, hands, boots and props are hand-designed
  pixel maps written as ASCII in the generator source; bodies are rendered by the project's own paper-doll rig.
* Licence: project-owned original asset. No third-party attribution. No regime insignia on any new sprite (the
  border officer's cap badge is the Free City's bridge, the conductor's the railway's winged wheel).
* Deterministic: re-running the generators reproduces byte-identical PNGs and JSON (verified 2026-11-06: all 23
  sheets rebuilt into a scratch directory, 0 differing files; `sheet.json` is now always written with LF).

### Reproduce
```bash
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters.py               # the 16 round-1/2 sheets (+ previews)
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters_c3.py            # the 7 Chapter-3 sheets, then both validators
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters_c3.py --check    # validators only (characters_check + C3 contract)
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/characters.py --out=DIR     # review run (writes DIR/assets/characters)
```

### What changed
| Item | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Chapter-3 generator (`chargen_cast_c3.py`, `characters_c3.py`) | written against the round-1 rig (`digit_legend`, `faces`, `hand_bitmaps`, first-gen portrait API) -- did not import any more, the sheets were frozen round-1 art | ported to the round-2 API: chargen_heads-format heads (`HD.head_views`, side + front, blink / talk / glance-back variants), `make_hands` (Kessler: gloves), round-2 materials / textures (tweed, knit, fur) |
| Chapter-3 rows | 6-frame idle, 4-frame talk, 2-4 rows, front view only for 3 of 7 | 9 rows each: `idle_side` (8, two-stage breathing), `walk_side`, `talk_side` (8: two beats + nod, per-frame hands / head), `idle_front`, `blink_side`, `look_side` (glance back), `gesture_side`, `blink_front`, `talk_front`; the first-contract rows keep their old order |
| Chapter-3 gestures | none | Elena tucks a strand behind her ear, the officer rocks on his heels, the conductor touches his cap, Voronova straightens her collar, Kessler straightens up, Frau Brunner reads her pad, Ekdahl jots a note (`chargen_anim.GESTURES`: `tuck_hair`, `touch_cap`, `straighten`, `read_pad`, `jot`) |
| Voronova | grey checker "karakul" that read as grey hair | dark brown karakul pirozhok with curl tips, grey hair under it, grey-brown karakul collar |
| Front long coats (Elena, Kessler) | lower front lit like an apron | calmer hem below the waist (`calm_front_hem`) |
| `talk_front` (all NPCs with a front view) + `talk_back` (protagonists) | did not exist (`talk_front` fell back to `talk_side`) | 8 frames: the free forearm rises in front of the body with an open hand, beat + nod, talking mouth; back view: the elbow moves out (a hand in front of the body would be hidden); per character `talk_front_arm` keeps props in the other hand (Berlin father: nod only) |
| `walk_front` / `walk_back` | planted foot travelled 0.5-2 px up in depth -> the whole figure floated 1-2 px above the origin mid-stride | the supporting foot stays on the origin row for the whole stance, frames 0 / 4 are double support (validator now checks these rows too) |

New rows are **appended** to the round-2 sheets (protagonists: rows 13-14, NPCs: last row), so every existing row
keeps its index (verified: only `walk_front` / `walk_back` of Alexei / Konrad changed pixels; the leaflet
mini-game's Konrad crop, the hall people and the cinematics read rows by name).

### Files — `assets/characters/<id>/` (cell 32x56, origin [16,54], facing right)
| id | rows | height | notes |

### Runtime (round 7)
* `scripts/actors/sprite_rig.gd`: `view_hint` ("front" / "back": `*_side` requests play the matching view row if
  the sheet has it -- dialogue code keeps asking for `talk_side` / `idle_side`); `turn_to(left, hold, row)` with an
  in-between row (`walk_front` keeps the stride), the turn frame survives row changes, turning back mid-turn cancels;
  `talk_back` falls back to `idle_back`.
* `scripts/actors/player.gd`: soft turns (0.12 s on the spot, 0.07 s while walking, movement still reverses at
  once; `face(dir, false)` = instant), `face_point()` turns towards the NPC named by the interactable's `npc_id`
  (oblique levels: towards / away from the camera when it stands clearly below / above), `_set_dir` keeps the rig's
  `view_hint` in step; `get_view()`.
* `scripts/actors/npc.gd`: front-on idles (`idle_anim = "idle_front"`) talk front-on unless they turn to the
  player; talk release accepts any talk row; wanderers turn softly while pausing at the ends of their path.

### Tests
`tests/test_characters_r7.gd` (unit, run_all): C3 rows / frames / variants / normal maps, talk_front everywhere a
front view exists, contract row indices, front / back walks grounded in every frame, living portraits for all 17
speakers, rig view hint + turns, player soft turn + facing view, front-on NPC talk.

## Asset manifest — dialogue portraits, round 7 (seventeen individual faces, Chapter-3 portraits alive)

Area owner: characters specialist · Date: **2026-11-06, round 7** · Engine target: Godot 4.7.2
Extends `portraits.md` (round 2; pipeline, file formats and runtime unchanged).

### Provenance & licence (applies to every file below)
* **Original work, generated procedurally** (numpy + Pillow; no AI image models, no network, no image inputs).
  Facial features are hand-designed pixel stamps / parametric strokes in the generator source.
* Licence: project-owned original asset. No regime insignia in any portrait (the officer's cap badge is the Free
  City's bridge, the conductor's the railway's winged wheel).
* Deterministic: verified 2026-11-06 (elena, babushka, porter rebuilt into a scratch directory: byte-identical).

### Reproduce
```bash
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py --jobs=9          # all 17 speakers, one process each (~2.5 min)
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py elena kessler     # some speakers
py -3 -P tools/pixelgen/chargen_portrait2.py --out=DIR         # review run into another assets/characters dir
```

### What changed
| Item | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Chapter-3 portraits (elena, border_officer, conductor, voronova, kessler, typist, journalist) | first-generation ellipsoid busts from a module that no longer imported; static strips, no talking mouth, no blink; a flatter, different look | `chargen_portrait_c3.py` registers the seven in `chargen_portrait2_cast.CAST`: same SDF pipeline as the rest, 4 expressions x 6 states (`portrait_anim.png` + anim block), backgrounds `veltrava` (slate dawn) and `palais` (navy-teal) |
| Faces | every speaker on the same sculpted base: same nose, chin, eye stamps and brow stroke | per speaker: head proportions and 3/4 angle, nose (length, projection, width, bump / hook / button / drooping tip), chin and jaw (pointed, square, receding, cleft, double), eye shapes (`chargen_portrait2_face.eye_shapes`: hooded / narrow / round / droop / lift / deep), brow geometry (`features['brow_pts']`, thickness, arch), nostril style; softer planes for the women (`SOFT`) |
| Babushka | headscarf wrapped like a hood over forehead, ears and cheeks | `kerchief_chin`: front edge at the hairline with a plain rolled hem, open face framed down the cheeks to a knot with two tails under the chin, back corner over the nape |
| Peaked caps (porter, officer, conductor) | the head clip reached 1.4 px above the crown -> hair showed through the cap top as a pale patch | clip ends at the crown top |
| Porter | no moustache (his sprite has one) | white moustache, hooded eyes, bushy white brows |

Speaker notes (face, in one line each): **Alexei** broad Slavic face, high cheekbones, once-broken nose, tired
hooded eyes, heavy low brows · **Konrad** narrow long face, long straight nose, defined chin, fine brows, more in
profile · **Irina** shorter rounder face, short slightly upturned nose, strong straight brows, wide eyes · **Emil**
bulbous nose, jowls, kind drooping hooded eyes, bushy grey brows · **Vendor** broad round face, button nose, soft
double chin, crinkled warm eyes, a ready smile · **Worker** flat broad nose, squint, heavy low brows ·
**Babushka** long thin nose with a drooping tip, small pointed chin, deep-set kind eyes · **Librarian** thin long
nose, narrow chin, hooded reading eyes, fine high brows · **Porter** fleshy, white moustache · **Photographer**
hooked nose, wry squint · **Elena** slim oval face, fine nose, small chin, lifted eyes, fine arched brows ·
**Officer** broad jowly face, short wide nose, hooded narrow eyes, trimmed dark moustache · **Conductor** long
gaunt face, drooping nose, receding chin under a walrus moustache, droopy eyes · **Voronova** broad face, square
jaw, short broad nose, level brows, steady hooded eyes, grey waves under the karakul · **Kessler** narrow ascetic
face, aquiline nose, pointed chin, thin lips, narrow eyes behind thin rimless glasses · **Frau Brunner** round
face, upturned small nose, round eyes, raised arched brows, a smile · **Ekdahl** long Nordic face, strong straight
nose, cleft chin, almost invisible fair brows.
